NQ: Q2 revised to forecast utilization

Research by Wan ho.Lui · 2022-01-03 to 2026-10-15 · version 2

Return to SessionPath

The active definition now compares each side’s percentage of its own Mean forecast. The five-minute candle-close method is archived unchanged. Its old U90/D90 labels do not apply to this revision.

Definition

U usage = max(session high − decision open, 0) ÷ forecast U distance.
D usage = max(decision open − session low, 0) ÷ forecast D distance.

Multiply by 100 to display percentages. Larger usage is dominant; equal usage is a tie. Wicks count. Usage above 100% remains uncapped. Missing, zero or negative forecast distances are excluded; a real zero excursion is retained.

Example: forecast U = $1.00, D = $0.50; actual U = $0.80, D = $0.45. U usage = 80%, D usage = 90%: D dominates, although the upward move is larger in points.

Bands: 0–<25%, 25–<50%, 50–<75%, 75–<100%, 100%+. The selected main-site lookup matches both the four usage bands and the two dominant sides. The full 625-cell usage tables are marginal band summaries, so equal-band cells may contain both dominance directions.

Windows use all complete five-minute bars in [03:00,05:00), [05:00,08:30), and [08:30,16:00), New York time. Each window uses its actual opening price. No endpoint-opening candle is included.

What changed?

WindowComparable datesLabels changedChanged %
three114814512.6%
five114914913.0%
ny11251109.8%

The paired comparison uses only dates with both the archived close-count measure and a valid new utilization measure. The combined NY baseline additionally requires all three valid windows; its denominator is therefore different.

The source contains 1249 dated entries. The primary combined Own Wide sample has 1087 eligible dates. 746 date/window/variant records are excluded for incomplete windows, unavailable earlier forecasts or nonpositive forecast sides; exclusions are not unique dates.

Four NY methods stay separate because their U/D forecast widths differ. Mean box utilization is a different outcome from final-close direction or the amount of time spent above the open.

Chronological comparison

On each test date, the baseline and conditional models predict the same U/D/tie utilization outcome. The first 250 eligible dates initialize the estimate; subsequent probabilities use earlier dates only, with 20 baseline-equivalent observations for shrinkage. Forecast widths themselves also use only earlier dates. Sparse fits remain flagged and are not replaced with future-history fits.

Negative differences favor conditioning; positive differences favor the baseline. These tested variants do not show an established predictive improvement from the new labels. This is a result of these models, not a proof that no other usable relationship exists.

Intervals use 1,000 circular bootstrap replicates of blocks of 10 eligible observations. They are unadjusted for exploring multiple variants and conditions. This history has already been explored and is not an untouched holdout.

Reproducibility and archive

Daily calculations · All 2,500 usage cells across four NY methods · Observed usage + dominant-side cells · Original candle-close observations and results

Counts include genuine zero moves. Boundary touching is part of the observed excursion; it is not a close-return event. All probabilities count one eligible date per chosen method, not one candle or one forecast variant as an independent date.

Excluded record reasonRecords
No eligible earlier forecast493
Incomplete five-minute window252
Missing or nonpositive forecast side1

Primary denominators require valid earlier forecasts and therefore differ from the original occupancy-only study. This prevents the revision from using future observations to construct historical boxes.

Original candles, the broader seven-study outputs, the five-minute close labels, the workbook and the review manuscripts remain preserved. The existing full-study manuscript describes v1 candle occupancy; this report supersedes its Q2 interpretation for utilization. Other research questions are not redefined here.
